Challenge these proposals under ONLY the supplied task rubric. Evaluate each criterion pass/fail/unknown with score 0–5 and a concrete rationale. Use unknown for evidence gaps, fail for established constraint violations. Entailment concerns reported facts, not proof of an explicitly untested prediction. An unmeasured bottleneck can be the first falsification question; do not demand a proven speedup before proposing a test. No-GPU-execution constraints prohibit launching jobs in this research session, not describing a later bounded experiment. A full-text source existing does not mean the quoted passage supports the claim. No search hits is uncertainty. Novelty is irrelevant unless explicitly enabled by the rubric. Score 0–5 with explicit objections. High polish must not rescue a failed gate. Known techniques, feature activation and ports are legitimate candidates if useful relative to the baseline. An explicit cheap baseline/eligibility check can resolve unknown deployment inclusion before implementation. Do not repeat historical novelty rejection as a current veto. Review each ID exactly once.

The factor-of-2 introduced by the identity is resolved by folding\n0.5f\ninto the existing\nscale_kv\nmultiply \u2014 zero extra instructions, bit-exact numerics.\nBackground\nOn SM90,\nfmaxf(x, 0)\ncompiles to a single FMNMX, which issues at 64 ops/clk/SM \u2014 half the rate of FADD/FFMA (128 ops/clk/SM). Meanwhile\nx + |x|\ncompiles to a single FADD with the absolute-value operand modifier, which is a free encoding bit in the instruction word:\nFADD R3, R3, |R3|    ; one instruction, full rate (cuobjdump, sm_90a)\nThe relu executes once per\n(token, head, kv)\nscore element in the MQA logits epilogue, so at 64 vs 128 ops/clk/SM the FMNMX form doubles the FP32 pipe time spent on relu alone.\nSince\nx + |x| = 2 \u00b7 relu(x)\nholds exactly for all finite IEEE-754 floats (\n+0\nfor x \u2264 0), the doubled sum is simply halved by folding\n* 0.5f\ninto the already-existing\nscale_kv\nmultiplication (an exponent decrement, also exact):\n//\nBefore:\nreturn\nfmaxf(shifted_accum[j],\n0\n) * weights[i][(j /\n4\n) *\n2\n+ (j &\n1\n)];\n...\nfloat\nv_0 = (sum[\n0\n] + sum[\n1\n]) * scale_kv_0;\n//\nAfter:\nreturn\n(shifted_accum[j] + fabsf(shifted_accum[j])) * weights[i][(j /\n4\n) *\n2\n+ (j &\n1\n)];\n...\nfloat\nv_0 = (sum[\n0\n] + sum[\n1\n]) * (scale_kv_0 *\n0\n.\n5f\n);\nThe same technique is already used by the SM100 kernel (\nsm100_mqa_logits.cuh\n, packed\nFADD2\nvariant); this PR brings it to SM90. Verified via\ncuobjdump -sass\n: zero FMNMX across all JIT-instantiated\nsm90_fp8_mqa_logits\ncubins.\nPerformance (H200, 132 SMs, SM90A)\nProduction indexer shapes, best of 3 reps, before \u2192 after:\nShape\nBefore\nAfter\nGain\nH=32, D=128, SQ=512, SKV=140k, FP32 logits\n1018 TFLOPS\n1093 TFLOPS\n+7.4%\nSQ=512, SKV=130560, H=64, BF16, CP=1\n1042 TFLOPS\n1126 TFLOPS\n+8.1%\nSQ=512, SKV=130560, H=32, BF16, CP=0\n941 TFLOPS\n989 TFLOPS\n+5.1%\nSQ=2048, SKV=8192, H=64, BF16, CP=1\n980 TFLOPS\n1027 TFLOPS\n+4.8%\nFull\ntests/test_attention.py\nsweep (192 contiguous MQA cases on SM90):\navg +3.7%\n(SK=65536 subset +4.4%, H=64 subset +5.5%, best case +11.3%). It can then\nread tensors before the epilogue's GPU work is complete. The same issue applies\nto deterministic sorting registered as an after-wait hook.\n\nThe fix keeps the original event and its retained tensors alive, records a\nseparate native `EventHandle` after the hook succeeds, and includes it in later\nwaits. Explicit `release_handle=True` releases both event handles. Hooks still\nrun once and only the first wait returns their result. A failing hook remains\nretryable. The ordinary no-hook path records no additional CUDA event. No\nhost/device synchronization is added.\n\n## Regression coverage\n\nThe current diff includes a standalone two-function CUDA regression in\n`tests/utils/test_event.py`:\n\n- A deliberately delayed hook writer and a second-stream reader, with a raw\n  original-event negative control, repeated waits, single-hook execution and\n  explicit event-handle release. The test requires the delayed write to still\n  be pending when the negative control completes; an ineffective window fails.\n- A no-hook wait and context-manager release, checking that no extra epilogue\n  event is recorded.\n\nRun it in an installed/built checkout:\n\n```bash\npython tests/utils/test_event.py\n```\n\n### Historical extended harness\n\nThe earlier standalone CUDA regression harness, retained in the tested commit\n[`e15813d`](https://github.com/yuweih205/DeepEP/blob/e15813df91be6a4288b280b8cb26a5e84b96c0e0/tests/utils/test_event.py), checks:\n\n- Two subsequent reader streams after a deliberately delayed writer hook,\n  with and without explicit release.\n- Context-manager waits after a prior hook on a different stream.\n- Multi-stream CUDA Graph capture and three replays.\n- No extra event capture when no hook is registered.\n- Hook failure/retry and first-wait return semantics.\n\nIts eager race tests require the delayed write to still be pending before the\nreader is queued. Otherwise they fail as an ineffective race test. They drain\nboth streams even if an assertion fails. This historical five-method harness\nis not the current two-function regression.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Current polished revision\n\nHead: `ebaaab5f4b58d3fd96ccbc9ba66481ba41cd1703`, based on public main\n`b5a33eefda39485f438dc764695b71ba7b9249b6`.\n\nBoth current CUDA regression functions passed on H200 with PyTorch\n`2.13.0+cu132`, CUDA 13.2 and NCCL 2.32.3. "github:deepseek-ai/DeepEP:780", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepEP/pull/780", "title": "Document and test ordinary sync-free receive tensor lifetimes", "created_at": "2026-10-15T07:55:01Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-17T08:12:01Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:22Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "content_hash": "3df7f7b248f78d308d19939f4a63095b2197b43556392700696419c16c1e636f", "body": "## Summary\n\nDocument and exercise the lifetime contract of **ordinary independently allocated receive outputs** from compact `dispatch(do_cpu_sync=False)`.\n\n- Add a README paragraph explaining valid receive prefixes, output ownership versus stream readiness, the explicit ready-event/`record_stream` handoff, and why a prefix view retains the full capacity allocation.\n- Add a 166-line standalone native regression for saved-view backward after later dispatches and delayed cross-stream consumers under allocator pressure.\n\nNo production Python/C++/CUDA implementation, default, or API changes. The internal dispatch copy epilogue still runs. This is not borrowed communication-buffer storage or a zero-copy dispatch implementation.\n\n## Regression coverage\n\nThe new `tests/ep/test_recv_lifetime.py` uses the independent NCCL dispatch reference and requires bitwise equality of payloads, local expert indices, routing weights, source-token metadata, and the saved-activation weight gradient.\n\nEach process run covers:\n\n- Deterministic dispatch off/on; output allocation on the compute/communication stream.\n- Uneven inputs, balanced routes, masked routes, and highly skewed routing with empty receivers.\n- Two selected experts on the same destination rank, distinguishing unique receive rows from token/expert assignments.\n- A prefix saved with `ctx.save_for_backward`, followed by three additional dispatches through the same buffer before backward.\n- A delayed reader on another stream, dropping the original receive allocation references and allocating/filling four same-capacity tensors on the allocation stream. The copy destination and allocator warm-up are prepared before the delay. Pressure writes must finish while a **pre-read** event is still pending; a pending post-copy event alone is insufficient.\n\nScheduling can miss the pressure window. All ranks agree on numerical correctness and window validity, then retry in collective lockstep with a longer delay (at most three attempts). Actual corruption fails immediately; an inconclusive window is never silently reported as coverage.\n\nThe consumer handoff uses a CUDA event captured **after the first dispatch-event wait**, including its deterministic-sort hook. It does not depend on repeated waits on the original `EventOverlap`; that is separate from this lifetime coverage.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Current polished revision\n\nHead: `97fa6d18ff717df99acbdbb862325bda3d4a0d46`, based on public main `b5a33eefda39485f438dc764695b71ba7b9249b6`.\n\nThe current 166-line test passed on H200 with PyTorch `2.13.0+cu132`, CUDA 13.2 and NCCL 2.32.3. Each native lifetime policy was run once with eight ranks: 24 distributed saved-prefix/backward cases in total, with 24 valid delayed-consumer allocator-pressure windows. This is positive coverage of the current reorganized test; the historical negative and scheduling controls below were not rerun against this revision.\n\n```bash\nEP_AVOID_RECORD_STREAM=0 python tests/ep/test_recv_lifetime.py --num-processes 8 --num-tokens 64 --hidden 512\nEP_AVOID_RECORD_STREAM=1 python tests/ep/test_recv_lifetime.py --num-processes 8 --num-tokens 64 --hidden 512\n```\n\n### Historical validation of the earlier test version\n\nTested commit: `01c31d0d75f155ca00ba933a6b9ab7a01459169e`, based on upstream `93eb6eb238127e96c6d7a4a625a6dad158348509`. The results in this subsection belong to that earlier test version, not to a rerun of the current 166-line test.\n\nOne node, eight NVIDIA H200 GPUs in a single NVLink domain; PyTorch `2.13.0+cu132`, CUDA runtime 13.2, loaded NCCL 2.32.3. The unchanged upstream native extension and the tested source archive/test-file hashes were verified. No borrowed-view implementation or event-wrapper patch was included.\n\nBoth native lifetime policies were run in fresh processes, three repeats each, using the same two command lines above.\n\nValidation controls: `EP_DISABLE_GIN=1`, `EP_REUSE_NCCL_COMM=0`, `EP_BUFFER_DEBUG=0`, `NCCL_NET_PLUGIN=none`, `CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG=:4096:8`. Today, we are excited to introduce\nDeepEP V2\n, which includes a complete refactoring of Expert Parallelism \u2014 achieving extreme performance with several times fewer SM resources compared to V1, while supporting significantly larger scale-up and scale-out domains \u2014 as well as experimental 0 SM Engram, 0 SM Pipeline Parallelism, and 0 SM Context Parallelism all-gather.\nWe are also happy to announce that we have switched from the NVSHMEM backend to the more lightweight\nNCCL Gin backend\n.\nNew Features\nFully JIT (Just-In-Time compilation)\nNCCL Gin backend\nHeader-only & lightweight\nAble to reuse existing NCCL communicators\nEPv2\nCUDA graph compatible, or faster CPU sync in saving memory mode\nHigh-throughput and low-latency APIs unified into a single interface, with a new GEMM layout\nLarger scale-up & scale-out domain support (up to EP2048)\nAnalytical SM & QP count calculation \u2014 no more auto-tuning needed\nBoth hybrid & direct modes remain supported\nFor V3-like legacy training, SM usage reduced from 24 to 4 - 6 while maintaining equivalent or better performance\n0 SM Engram (with RDMA)\n0 SM PP (with RDMA)\n0 SM CP (with Copy Engine)\nNotes\nBuffer size consumption is larger than V1\n0 SM RDMA low-latency EP is no longer supported\nEngram, PP, and CP are experimental features\nStill On-going Features\nElastic GPU & CPU buffers\n: A contiguous virtual address space that maps to a hybrid of GPU and CPU physical memory under the hood, enabling fully automatic and transparent Engram or imbalanced EP\nReducing intermediate buffer sizes by leveraging EP replay to handle load imbalance\nAll-gather updates and reduce-scatter implementations for DP & TP\nPerformance\nFollowing V3's configuration, we tested with 8K tokens per batch, 7168 hidden dimensions, top 8 experts, FP8 dispatching, and BF16 combining, and obtained the following results:\nArch\nNIC type\nTopo\nDispatch Bottleneck Bandwidth\nCombine Bottleneck Bandwidth\n#SMs\nSM90\nCX7\nEP 8 x 2\n90 GB/s (RDMA)\n81 GB/s (RDMA)\n12\nSM90\nCX7\nEP 8 x 4\n61 GB/s (RDMA)\n61 GB/s (RDMA)\n6\nSM100\nCX7\nEP 8 x 2\n90 GB/s (RDMA)\n91 GB/s (RDMA)\n12\nSM100\nN/A\nEP 8\n726 GB/s (NVLink)\n740 GB/s (NVLink)\n64 (Max perf)\nSM100\nN/A\nEP 8\n643 GB/s (NVLink)\n675 GB/s (NVLink)\n24 (Min #SM)\nNotes, the results are logical bandwidth. "github:deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM:474", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepGEMM/pull/474", "title": "[Feat]Add native FP8 Tensor Core GEMM support for SM89 (Ada Lovelace)", "created_at": "2026-10-14T19:01:11Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-16T18:30:02Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:23Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "content_hash": "e9e726ee03fe75aa494d2982073d7cff644a759f6b2dc7cbce2a25919d9f2557", "body": "\n**Title**  \nAdd native FP8 Tensor Core GEMM support for SM89 (Ada Lovelace)\n\n**Body**\n\n## Summary\n\nThis change adds a native FP8 Tensor Core GEMM for SM89 (Ada Lovelace). SM89 is the first architecture below Hopper that has FP8 Tensor Cores.\n\nPreviously, SM80 and SM89 used the same path. The path required SM90 or higher for FP8. This was correct for SM80 because Ampere has no FP8 Tensor Cores. It was wrong for SM89. SM89 implements the instruction `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k32.row.col.f32.e4m3.e4m3.f32` (`QMMA.16832.F32.E4M3.E4M3`). The change separates the two architectures and gives SM89 a real kernel.\n\n## Why\n\n- SM89 has FP8 Tensor Cores. It does not have the SM90 features (no TMA, no `mbarrier.try_wait.parity`, no WGMMA, no clusters). It could not reuse the SM90 or SM100 kernels. It fell back to BF16 through cuBLASLt.\n- FP8 users on Ada had to dequantize and run BF16. They lost about 2\u00d7 of Tensor Core throughput.\n- The shared SM80/SM89 path also gave wrong error messages. An SM89 user saw a message that did not apply to their GPU.\n\n## Design\n\nSM89 has no TMA and no asynchronous barriers. The kernel uses the pre-Hopper primitives.\n\n- It uses `mma.sync` (wrapped in `deep_gemm/mma/sm89.cuh`, with A/B/C fragment layouts documented) and `ldmatrix.x4` for operand staging.\n- It uses a multi-stage `cp.async` pipeline with a 128-byte XOR swizzle.\n- It uses a persistent block scheduler (one CTA per SM). Tiles walk in a loop.\n- It uses direct global stores because SM89 has no TMA store. It has an alignment-safe scalar tail. An MMA lane owns a pair of adjacent columns. For odd N the element offset of a row can be odd. A 2-element vector store is then illegal. This caused the original misaligned address failure.\n- Block K is fixed at 128 because of the per-128-channel scaling granularity. Pipeline depth and tile shapes come from a new cost model in `csrc/jit_kernels/heuristics/sm89.hpp`. The warp split uses one shared function `get_mma_sync_warps_n()`. Host heuristics and device code both call it so they cannot disagree.\n\nBoth SM90 SF recipes are supported:  \n`(1, 1, 128)` (per-token A and B scales) and  \n`(1, 128, 128)` (per-token A, one B scale per 128-column N block).  \nThe second recipe is the default when FP32 scales are used. SM89 uses the same FP32 SF layouts as SM90. The function `transform_sf_into_required_layout` was extended. It was not duplicated.\n\n## Scope\n\nSupported features:  \ndense FP8 GEMM, K-major (NT) operands only, FP32 and BF16 outputs, `D = C + A @ B`, ragged M/N, `k % 16 == 0`, both aliases (`fp8_fp4_gemm_nt`, `fp8_gemm_nt`, `fp4_gemm_nt`), and shape specialization.\n\nStill rejected (with an explicit message that names the requirement):  \ngrouped and K-grouped FP8, MN-major FP8 operands, FP4, custom epilogues, `alpha` scaling, PDL, and MQA/attention.  \nBF16 keeps its cuBLASLt fallback.\n\n## Incidental fixes\n\n- `set_pdl()` now panics below SM90. It no longer sets a flag that `griddepcontrol` cannot honour.  \n  `cudaGridDependencySynchronize()` is wrapped in a no-op below `__CUDA_ARCH__ >= 900` so the SF-layout kernels compile for SM89.\n- `call_cublaslt_api` used `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F_FAST_TF32` for non-FP32 operands. That mode only down-casts FP32 inputs to TF32. BF16/FP8 operands ran at TF32 precision by mistake. Now only FP32 inputs use that mode.\n- A new `DG_PANIC` macro throws always and carries a message.\n\n## Bug found during validation\n\nThe persistent loop primed its `cp.async` pipeline only in the prologue before the tile loop. It relied on a cross-tile tail load to prime the next tile. A tile\u2019s tail can only reach the stages that are already free of the current tile \u2014 prologue stages `p \u2208 [0, kNumStages-2-num_k_blocks]`. The next tile\u2019s K loop consumes `p = 0..num_k_blocks-1`. When `num_k_blocks < kNumStages-1` (example: `k=512`, 4 blocks, 8 stages) the high stages were never reloaded. The next tile read the previous tile\u2019s shared memory. Symptom: non-deterministic `nan` and values near 1e30. The problem appeared only for small M spread over many N tiles.\n\nThe fix extracts the prologue into `prime_pipeline()` and re-primes unconditionally after the epilogue. The re-prime is guarded by `cp_async_wait<0>()` and a `__syncthreads()`. `cp.async` completion is per-thread, so both are required before the stages can be reused. The change removes a prologue/epilogue overlap that was never correct. Measured cost is about 0\u20133.5 % (4096\u00b3: 2518 \u00b5s vs 2507 \u00b5s, within noise; 4096\u00d74096\u00d7512: 420 \u00b5s vs 405 \u00b5s).\n\n`tests/test_sm89.py` added the shapes `(1, 4096, 512)`, `(64, 4096, 512)` and `(128, 4096, 128)`. Reverting the fix makes these tests fail (34 failures). They are real regression tests.\n\n## Testing\n\nHardware: RTX 4060 Laptop (SM89, 24 SMs, 7.6 GiB), CUDA 13.0, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130.\n\n- `tests/test_sm89.py` \u2014 17 tests, OK from a cold JIT cache (~130 s).  \n  Covers both recipes \u00d7 both output dtypes \u00d7 accumulate \u00d7 12 shapes, explicit `recipe=(1,128,128)`, `recipe_a`/`recipe_b` pairs, entry-point aliases, c", "body_excerpted": The new\npath is explicitly restricted to SM90 and is not enabled automatically.\nNote\nAlternative host-offload path (not part of this pr):\nThe packed interface also enables a separate offload path in which gathering and packing are performed on the host, followed by transferring only the packed selected-KV workspace to the device. In this case, the full KV caches of the shared layers no longer need to remain resident in HBM. This can substantially expand KV-cache capacity.\nSince this implementation introduces a compile-time packed-KV specialization in the existing SM90 sparse decode kernel, I\u2019d appreciate maintainers\u2019 guidance on whether you prefer the current shared-kernel approach, a policy/loader-based abstraction, or a separate packed-kernel implementation; I\u2019m happy to restructure it in a follow-up revision based on your feedback.\nMotivation\nGLM-5.2 can share selected physical indices across multiple MLA layers. When\nthose indices are available before the layer that consumes them, the runtime\ncan move sparse address resolution and KV gathering into an earlier overlap\nwindow.\nThe attention kernel can then read a contiguous packed workspace instead of\nrepeatedly:\nloading and validating physical indices;\ntranslating indices into page and in-page coordinates;\nconstructing unrelated global addresses; and\ngathering NoPE, scale, and RoPE components from the paged cache.\nPacking and attention are intentionally separate operations so a runtime can\noverlap packing with preceding work such as MoE communication.\nWhat changed\nSM90 packing kernel\npack_selected_kv\ncopies each selected 656-byte V3.2 record byte-for-byte:\n512-byte FP8 E4M3 NoPE data;\n16-byte FP32 scales; and\n128-byte BF16 RoPE data.\nThe packer writes an opaque 64-token tile-major workspace. It supports a valid\nselected-index prefix described by\ntopk_length\nand zero-fills invalid suffix\nrecords.\nCallers may provide reusable contiguous\nuint8\nstorage using\nget_packed_kv_workspace_size\n.\nPacked attention specialization\nThe packed H=64 and H=128 instantiations reuse FlashMLA's existing:\nWGMMA and dequantization pipeline;\nonline softmax;\nDSM clustering for H=128;\nsplit accumulation;\nepilogue; and\ncombine kernel.\nThe specialization replaces the sparse index/page/gather loader with direct\ntile-major loads and preserves partial-tail masking.\nScheduler policy\nH=128 uses the same scheduler as native sparse decode.\nH=64 uses the compact request-local scheduler selected by the L20X\nexperiments. For the measured full-prefix\ntopk=2048\n, 132-SM\nB*S_q=16\nregime, it falls back to the faster native scheduler.\nThis scheduler is used only by the opt-in packed helper and does not change\nnative sparse decode. The benchmark includes an identical-scheduler control to\nisolate the packed-load kernel gain.\nPython API\nThis PR adds:\nget_packed_kv_workspace_size\nget_packed_mla_metadata\npack_selected_kv\nflash_mla_with_packed_kvcache\nSupported scope\nThe initial path is intentionally limited to:\nSM90;\nV3.2 FP8 sparse KV records;\nd_qk=576\n;\nd_v=512\n;\nH in {64, 128}\n;\nMQA KV; and\ntop-k capacity divisible by 64.\nThe API fails closed for unsupported architectures and shapes. SM100 is not\nenabled because this implementation has only been evaluated on SM90.\nL20X results\nThe repository-local benchmark was run on an NVIDIA L20X with 132 SMs using:\nCUDA 13.0;\nPyTorch 2.13.0;\ntopk=2048\n;\nB={1,2,4,7,8}\n;\nS_q={1,2,3,4,8,16}\n;\nH={64,128}\n;\nrequest-disjoint physical KV ranges;\na 512 MiB L2 flush before each measured path;\n10 warmups and 200 timed repetitions; and\n60 total cases.\nComparison\nGeometric-mean speedup\nWins\nPacked attention, identical scheduler\n1.098x\n60/60\nIntegrated packed attention stage\n1.115x\n60/60\nIntegrated H=64 cohort\n1.131x\n30/30\nH=128 cohort, native scheduler\n1.100x\n30/30\nPack + attention without overlap\n0.740x\n0/60\nPacking is excluded from the attention-stage comparisons.\nThe unhidden result is included deliberately: this path is useful only when\nthe runtime can move most packing work outside the attention critical path.\nThis PR does not add automatic dispatch or change the default native path.\nThe H=64 hybrid policy selected the tuned scheduler in 26 cases and the native\nfallback in four cases. The four fallback cases improved by approximately\n6.5% to 7.4% compared with using the tuned scheduler unconditionally.\nCorrectness and benchmark coverage\npython tests/test_", "body_excerpted": link\nCopy Markdown\nSummary\nThis PR adds optional per-request\ntopk_length\nsupport to the SM90\nDeepSeek-V3.2 FP8 sparse decode kernel (\nd_qk=576\n). SM90 V3.2 can now use\ndynamic top-k instead of always processing the full allocated top-k capacity.\nWhen\ntopk_length\nis provided, the kernel:\nloads the valid length for each batch row;\nschedules only\nmax(ceil_div(topk_length, TOPK_BLOCK_SIZE), 1)\nblocks;\nmasks a partial tail before its indices can be used for KV addressing; and\nincludes the absolute top-k position in the softmax validity predicate.\nWhen\ntopk_length == nullptr\n, the existing fixed top-k behavior is preserved.\nThere is no public API change, and MODEL1 and SM100 behavior are unchanged.\nWhy\ntemplate<bool DYNAMIC_TOPK, ...>\nI also tested a single-device-kernel implementation that checks\nparams.topk_length != nullptr\nat runtime. The condition is grid-uniform, but\nthe compiler cannot remove the length load, tail predicates, extra control\nflow, or their value lifetimes from the fixed path.\ntemplate<bool DYNAMIC_TOPK>\nmoves the choice to host launch dispatch:\nfalse\n: the dynamic-length logic is compiled out, preserving the fixed path;\ntrue\n: per-row block counts and partial-tail safety checks are enabled.\nThe comparison implementation is available at\n7b7c831\non\ntest/sm90-v32-dynamic-topk-runtime-device\n. It regresses the representative\nB64-B128 fixed cases by 8.4%-9.4%, while the specialized implementation stays\nwithin 0.5% of the baseline. Both builds report 168 registers, 12 barriers,\nand no spills, so the generated hot-path instructions\u2014not an occupancy\nchange\u2014explain the observed difference.\nCorrectness\npython tests/test_flash_mla_sparse_decoding.py\nFocused reference checks passed for:\nCase\nResult\nV3.2 dynamic H64 / H128\npass\nExisting MODEL1 dynamic H64 / H128\npass\nV3.2 tail lengths\n0,1,17,63,64,65,127,128\npass\nFor the tail-safety check, every suffix entry after\ntopk_length\nwas replaced\nwith\nINT_MAX\n. H64 and H128 matched the corresponding safe-tail results, with\nno illegal memory access.\nKernel performance\nEnvironment: NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3 (SM90), driver 580.126.09, nvcc 12.8.93,\nand PyTorch 2.13.0+cu129. Each number is the median of seven group means, with\n10 warmups and 20 timed calls per group using CUDA events. Scheduler metadata\nis reused and excluded from timing.\nThese are standalone FlashMLA sparse-decode measurements; they exclude input\ngeneration, index compaction, framework integration, collectives, and model\nexecution. No end-to-end speedup is claimed.\nCompared revisions:\nBuild\nRevision\nUpstream baseline\n15f13e5\nRuntime device check\n7b7c831\nFinal specialization\ncfc99c2\nFixed top-k regression check\nShape:\nS_q=2\n,\nH=128\n,\nd_qk=576\n,\ns_kv=32768\n, page block 64,\nvariable KV lengths,\ntopk=2048\n. Positive deltas are regressions.\nBatch\nBaseline (us)\nRuntime check (us)\nDelta\nSpecialized (us)\nDelta\n2\n25.10\n24.99\n-0.46%\n25.11\n+0.03%\n64\n172.93\n189.21\n+9.41%\n172.90\n-0.02%\n74\n214.30\n232.31\n+8.41%\n213.79\n-0.24%\n128\n339.07\n369.87\n+9.08%\n337.39\n-0.50%\nGeomean\n-\n-\n+6.53%\n-\n-0.18%\nFor B64-B128, the runtime-check version regresses by 8.97% geomean. The final\nspecialized version is -0.25% versus baseline, showing no fixed-path\nregression within measurement variation.\nDynamic top-k\nShape:\n[B=256, S_q=1, H=128, d_qk=576]\n,\ns_kv=4096\n, page block 64,\nallocated top-k 2048, effective per-row top-k 1024. It is\nalso 6.90% faster than the shared runtime-check dynamic path, while\nspecialization avoids the latter's 8.51% fixed-path regression in this shape.\nReproduction\nBuild each worktree sparse MLA cache format support\n\u2026\n2c71fb6\nDeepSeek-V4.1-Flash stores each KV token as 528B: 512B of FP8 E4M3 covering\nthe full 512-wide K (rope lanes quantized, no BF16 rope segment) plus a 16B\nfooter of 16 UE8M0 scales over 32-wide groups (the\ndeepseek-ai/FlashMLA#221\nlayout, SM100-only there). Add ModelType::DSV4_1 on top of the ScaleSpec\ngroundwork: GLM53_NOPE-style geometry (D_ROPE=0) composed with a 32-wide\nUE8M0 footer.\n\nKernel side the format is simpler than DSV4 (one 512B bulk per row, a single\nunified block-scaled FP8 QK pass, no rope MMA paths). The two new mechanics:\n\n- the 16B footer row generalizes the scale gather from one uint64_t to a\n  width-selected load (uint4) in both the prefill IO helper and the decode\n  kernel's staged gather;\n- the 32-wide groups constrain the XV W-fold (one scale group per W buffer),\n  so an 8-warp XV split would floor NT_PER_WARP_XV to 0: decode runs a\n  4-math-warp tile at BI=64 (the DOTS3_SWA halving precedent) and prefill is\n  SG-only on the BI=32 producer/consumer tile. The ComputeTraits/SmemLayout\n  aliases now take the XV warp count so the NT_PER_WARP_XV assert evaluates at\n  the warp count the XV MMA actually runs at.\n\nSelection is explicit everywhere (d_qk=512 collides with DSV4, the 528B\npayload with GLM53_NOPE): the functional API gains kv_scale_format=\n\"ue8m0_g32\", the TRTLLM-compat entry kv_cache_format=\"fp8_dsv41\", and the\ndecode-dsv4 FFI takes an explicit model_type (-1 keeps the legacy width\ninference, matching the paged-entry kAuto convention). Dual-cache decode is\nsupported like DSV4; dual-cache prefill (MG_DUAL) is intentionally not\ninstantiated. Calibration keys a new dsv4_1 family at topk 512.\n\nTests: decode matrix (dedicated/runtime-H heads, partial-tail topk, sinks),\nSG prefill, dual-cache through the TRTLLM entry, poisoned-slot-0 NaN safety\non both gather paths, dispatch/plan/envelope coverage. 666 passed on RTX PRO\n6000 (SM120).\n\nSigned-off-by: lucifer1004 "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/6422", "title": "[SDSA 3/7] Add paged MQA logits for an indexer with one head (BF16 keys)", "created_at": "2026-10-13T10:59:15Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-19T11:30:51Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:36Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "content_hash": "0c953ef924741006b447e59395c9df1dce6c9da4b047322d03e72f7c5d66ec92", "body": "DSA-style sparse attention uses an indexer to pick each query token's Top-K keys. `fp8_paged_mqa_logits` scores keys for an indexer with many heads. This PR adds `single_head_paged_mqa_logits` for an indexer with one head. Its score is the dot product of a query row and a key, with no head weights and no ReLU.\n\nThe keys are BF16, with 128 dimensions, in pages of 64 keys. Each request has 1 to 8 query rows, such as a decode token and its draft tokens. The kernel is a warp-specialized SM100 kernel. Its grid size comes from the context capacity, not the lengths, so the call can be captured in a CUDA graph.\n\nOn GB200 (NVFP4 keys, 16-128 requests, 32K-1M keys), this grid was up to 15% faster than a grid sized from the actual lengths. It was 3% slower in the worst case.\n\nPart 3 of 7 of SDSA. SDSA is sparse attention with a one-head indexer that picks each query token's Top-K keys, and an MQA core that attends to them. This stack adds it in 7 PRs: token-sparse attention for MQA/GQA (1-2), then the single-head indexer (3-7).\n\nPrevious: #6421 (2/7) Add key unions and masks so dense attention kernels can run token-sparse attention. A replica-deterministic sample sets a value\n  threshold and a 256-bin scale; every CTA walks its slice once; the last-arriving CTA merges\n  the bin-major publish tables and resolves the crossing bin with an exact key-space select.\n  Rows up to 16K columns never enter that pipeline: a census arm (<= 4K) and a register-resident\n  arm (4K to 16K) solve them in one CTA. Floods past the tie capacity are refined from the\n  crossing bin's exact key range; anything else falls through to a fused exact re-solve on\n  device, so every call stays sync-free and CUDA-graph capturable.\n- `radix_primitives` \u2014 SGLang's coarse-histogram structure made exact (no tie truncation),\n  fp32/fp16/bf16, grouped rows (`next_n`) and compressed columns (`compress_ratio`), optional\n  values, multi-CTA long rows.\n- `cutlass_primitives` \u2014 the vendored cutlass-primitives library (`VENDORED.md`, Apache-2.0):\n  register, clustered-register and streaming kernels with a census split for large k; the\n  full-row leader. `flashinfer.topk_varlen` exports its workspace helpers\n  (`cutlass_primitives_workspace_bytes`, `cutlass_primitives_row_order`) and\n  `release_cutlass_primitives_resources`.\n- `sglang` \u2014 SGLang's own DeepSeek-V4 indexer top-k (Apache-2.0, vendored verbatim from upstream\n  commit 7f27bf4708; `csrc/sglang_dsv4/VENDORED.md`, `licenses/LICENSE.sglang.txt`) under the\n  same contract, including a paged variant: the same kernel SGLang runs, for parity and for the\n  check that the FlashInfer API adds no overhead over the raw kernel. It is exact only while the\n  rank-k bin holds at most 2048 candidates, so `auto` never selects it.\n\n**Fused paged output.** `top_k_varlen(..., page_table=table, page_size=64)` returns physical\nKV slots `page_table[q, c // page_size] * page_size + c % page_size` from the selection launch\nitself (the SGLang DSA decode contract, one kernel instead of a top-k plus a transform pass).\nThe table is a contiguous, 16-byte-aligned int32 `(requests, max_pages)` tensor covering every\nrequest's valid length; it may be narrower than the logits width, and rows past its coverage\nare clamped to the covered prefix. Served by `walkfirst_primitives`, `radix_primitives` and\n`sglang`; `backend=\"auto\"` with a page table ranks the two exact primitives backends (they are\noutside the unpaged ranking).\n\n**Also in this PR:** a fix to the pre-existing multi-CTA `radix` kernel (chunks shorter than\ntheir misaligned prefix read past the valid length and wrote shared memory at negative offsets;\nCTA 0 could reset the group's arrival counter while a time-sliced peer still spun on it; the\nper-group state grows by one int, STATE_SIZE 770 to 771, allocated internally with a bumped\ncache key), the trace template for `top_k_varlen` extended with the paged inputs (the page size\nnames the paged definitions), the benchmarks that produced the numbers below, the four new\nbackends in `benchmarks/routines/topk_varlen.py`, and a one-line fix to\n`tests/trace/example.py` (an upstream call passed a `str` device to `is_sm100a_supported`, which\ncrashes the example on sm_100 hosts).\n\n**Why.** The DSA indexer runs one top-k per layer per decode step; SGLang today uses its own\nfused kernel. These backends are exact where that kernel approximates, faster on every decode\ncell we measured across six architectures, and fuse the page-table output so an engine needs\none launch. Measurements (`benchmarks/bench_topk_replay_ties.py`, CUDA-graph replay, 16 rows,\nK 512, fp32, rows from a recorded DeepSeek decode trace of 500 to 2100 columns in a 16K buffer;\n\"paged\" = fused page-table output on both sides; The main changes are:\nEnable Hopper FP4 kernels without requiring CUDA native FP4 headers on SM90\nby using CUTLASS subbyte FP4 types.\nAdd optimized mixed-input mainloops for post-MMA scaling and pre-MMA E8M0\nscale fusion.\nPrecompute grouped GEMM work scheduling and TMA descriptor state before the\nmain GEMM kernel.\nUse folded weight-scale storage so the weight-scale layout is independent of\nthe profiled\nTileK\n.\nAdd precomputed scheduler kernels for ping-pong and cooperative grouped GEMM\nschedulers.\nAdd runtime activation expansion and per-token FP8 quantization support for\nthe pre-MMA E8M0 scale-fusion path.\nAdd per-token scale epilogue callbacks for the FP8 activation + MXFP4 weight\npath.\nExtend autotune and tactic validation so Hopper mixed-input tactics are\nchecked for correctness before being selected.\nAdd and update tests for Hopper MXFP4xBF16, INT4xFP8, and MXFP4xFP8 pre-MMA scale\npaths.\n\ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\nN/A\nPerformance\nBenchmarks were run on NVIDIA H20 and H200 GPUs using MoE workload configurations matching DeepSeek-V3 (256 experts, top-8, hidden size 7168, intermediate size 2048) and DeepSeek-V4-Pro (384 experts, top-6, hidden size 7168, intermediate size 3072).\nhttps://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V3\nhttps://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro\nThe plots below summarize the PR performance results. On SM100 (`mqa_logits_use_mx_scales()`) the writers store that scale's exponent as four equal UE8M0 bytes instead of the fp32, which is an exact MXFP8 encoding of the same values (same cache size, same FP8 bytes):\n\n| Writer | Models |\n|---|---|\n| C++ `indexer_k_quant_and_cache` (`scale_fmt=\"mxfp8\"`) | DeepSeek-V2-class `Indexer` (V3.2 via that class, DeepSeek MTP), HY-V4, DeepSeek-V3.2 under PCP |\n| DeepSeek-V3.2 fused norm/RoPE (`_fused_norm_rope_kernel`) | DeepSeek-V3.2, GLM-5/5.2 (`GlmMoeDsa`), Dots3Note, V3.2 MTP |\n| DeepSeek-V4 compressor (Triton; the CuTeDSL compressor is head-512 MLA only) | DeepSeek-V4 |\n| DeepSeek-V4.1 `indexer_k_norm_rope_store` | DeepSeek-V4.1 |\n| GLM-5.3 kpool prefill compress + decode update | GLM-5.3 |\n\nReaders need no change: `cp_gather_indexer_k_quant_cache` (prefill, all of the above) and the DCP/PCP gathers copy the 4 scale bytes verbatim, and DeepGEMM reads the paged cache directly. The 512-wide MLA caches, MXFP4 caches, and the MiniMax-M3 / Qwen4 indexers are untouched.\n\nFP8 Q keeps folding its per-(token, head) power-of-two scale into `weights`; `mqa_logits_mx_inputs` passes unit MX Q scales (exact, since `relu(s\u00b7x) = s\u00b7relu(x)` for `s > 0`) and casts weights to BF16. \"VLLM_SPARSE_INDEXER_MAX_LOGITS_MB\": lambda: int(\n         os.getenv(\"VLLM_SPARSE_INDEXER_MAX_LOGITS_MB\", \"512\")\n     ),\n+    # Score only the candidate blocks in the DeepSeek V4.1 On SM100 that is DeepGEMM's sparse MQA-logits kernels.\n+SM90 has no equivalent, so the consumers score the whole compressed context\n+with the dense paged kernel and then mask the logits down to the candidate\n+blocks, discarding the work outside them. With a 16K-position candidate set\n+against a 1M-position context that is ~98% of the scoring.\n+\n+This module scores only the candidate positions, reading K **in place** from\n+the paged FP8 indexer cache. Logits land in a compact ``[rows, num_cands *\n+candidate_block_size]`` buffer whose columns are candidate-relative, the usual\n+decode top-k runs over that buffer, and the selected columns are remapped back\n+to request-local compressed positions.\n+\n+Two properties keep replays correct under full CUDA-graph capture:\n+\n+* **No per-step host decision.** Context lengths stay on the device; the host\n+  only sees shapes that are fixed by the model and scheduler config. A full\n+  CUDA graph therefore cannot bake a context-dependent choice, and replays\n+  stay correct at any live context length.\n+* **K is read in place.** Unlike a scheme that gathers candidate K into a\n+  scratch buffer first, no K data is copied that the dense path would not\n+  also read. The only extra work is per-row candidate bookkeeping (expansion\n+  and sorting of block ids), index arithmetic paid only while the path is\n+  active.\n+\n+Numerics follow the decode reference (``fp8_paged_mqa_logits``): dot, relu,\n+head weights, sum over heads, then one dequantization multiply per column.\n+Scaling after the relu is exact because the per-position scale is positive,\n+and it costs ``num_heads`` times fewer multiplies.\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+import torch\n+\n+from vllm.logger import init_logger\n+from vllm.model_executor.kernels.attention.dsa.sparse_mqa_logits import (\n+    _sparse_topk_remap_kernel,\n+    candidate_blocks_to_sparse_indices,\n+)\n+from vllm.model_executor.layers.indexer_topk import (\n+    RADIX_TOPK_WORKSPACE_SIZE,\n+    get_indexer_topk,\n+)\n+from vllm.triton_utils import tl, triton\n+from vllm.v1.worker.workspace import current_workspace_manager\n+\n+logger = init_logger(__name__)\n+\n+# One fp32 scale per cached position, stored after the block's value bytes.\n+INDEXER_SCALE_BYTES = 4\n+# The same value for use inside the kernel; Triton only reads globals that are\n+# instantiated as constexpr.\n+_SCALE_BYTES = tl.constexpr(4)\n+\n+# Compact columns per program. A multiple of the candidate block size, and at\n+# least 16 so the MMA has a full M tile.\n+_BLOCK_N = 128\n+\n+\n+@triton.jit\n+def _prepare_candidate_rows_kernel(\n+    seq_lens,\n+    row_ks,\n+    row_ends,\n+    rows,\n+    next_n: tl.constexpr,\n+    per_row_lens: tl.constexpr,\n+    BLOCK: tl.constexpr,\n+):\n+    \"\"\"Per-row causal context length, and the zero K-range start the paged\n+    candidate expansion expects (its block ids are context-relative).\"\"\"\n+    offsets = tl.program_id(0) * BLOCK + tl.arange(0, BLOCK", "body_excerpted": true, "metadata_excerpt": "{\"sha256\": \"36acafb05e1e8881138089415a2678e4652bca6bf45b82fba40d953663fcb172\", \"patch_commit_shas\": [\"57b53a590e1171c9d5dd7b4f5091320c17bf2edb\", \"1abc6a2dab831633f0f168cb26a206b9459e873b\", \"c7509a10e5740befa3eaa6d12f73cd7d88db6c92\"], \"snapshot\": \"/home/ubuntu/AutoResearch-Inference/ideation/runs/h200-fresh-research-001/primary/vllm-project--vllm--62463/changes.patch\"}", "metadata_excerpted": false}], "coverage": {"available": 38, "included": 38, "omitted_retrieval_ids": [], "meaning": "All packet and cited sources retained; additional retrieval is bounded.
